# Z0 scored task set record (stage 3), started 2026-10-16

Stage 3 of the prereg (`2026-09-29-z0-built-in-memory-prereg.md`, stage 3): the scored task set, authored after the freeze by blind authors and screened on the control arms. The design is `docs/evals/z0-freeze/sizes-a2x-e0.15-r10.json`: 10 repositories, each with 3 set R families, 3 set X families and 6 set N tasks. The task files stay outside this repository until the result is published; this record holds the rules, the repositories with their pins, the hash, the drop list and the overlap table.

## Pick rule, set before any test result was read

Set on 2026-10-16. A test worker started timing the candidates' test commands before this rule was written, but no result of it had been read when this section was committed.

- **Input.** `C:/z0-runs/scored-shortlist/candidates.json`, sha256 `5e4d0d228db19fedeabc3f958d804118ec2aa9809c822df354211c185b6aaa75`: 48 public repositories with their default-branch commit (the pin), language, licence, rule files, test command, and the rule lines a heuristic found checkable. The shortlist script (`build.py`) excludes the development repositories.
- **Eligible.** An OSI licence, at least 4 checkable rule lines, and a product that is not agent instruction files (this drops `Yeachan-Heo/oh-my-claudecode`).
- **Order.** Each eligible repository's sort key is the sha256 of `<candidates sha256>:<owner/name>`, ascending. No count of rule lines enters it. The shortlist heuristic counts modal words such as "never" and "always", and hippo's capture reads the same words, so ranking by that count could favour hippo.
- **Walk.** In that order, take a repository if it passes the test gate and its language is under the cap. Stop at 13: the first 10 are scored and 11 to 13 are spares, in order. The walk runs in tiers. The first tier needs 6 or more rule lines with a cap of 2 per language. If 13 are not found, the second tier raises the cap to 3. The third tier lowers the rule floor to 4. Each tier walks the same order and skips the repositories already decided.
- **Test gate.** The candidate's test command runs at the pin on this machine, once to warm the caches and once timed. It passes if the timed run takes 300 seconds or less and either exits 0, or has 20 or more passes with at most 2% failures. Both an upstream clone and a clone of the eval snapshot must pass; the snapshot's time is recorded. A command that cannot run as written on Windows gets the smallest faithful translation, made by the test worker, who never saw this rule; `testgate.json` records each translation.
- **Fewer than 10.** If the tiers end with fewer than 10 repositories, stage 3 stops and reports, since the go fixed 10.

`C:/z0-runs/scored/pick.mjs` (sha256 `5bc99b6cfd18d8fbb1893e0390fbb15d0078e06eb7f99e2d0b1b32a532ee060e`; the first version, `83a0bed6`, read the gate files as arrays and crashed on their keyed-object form before any pick, and only that reader changed) applies the rule to `testgate.json` and `snapshot-gate.json` and writes `picks.json`. `node C:/z0-runs/scored/pick.mjs --order-only` prints the order; its first 10 are linguist, compose, quivr, zotero-format-metadata, DeepSeek-Reasonix, apollo, comprehensive-rust, ant-design-pro, aspnetcore and rustfs.

## Split rule, set before any family was authored or screened

Set on 2026-10-16. The split uses family numbers only, never how strongly a family screened.

- **Numbers.** Each repository's families are numbered in authoring order, `<key>-f1` upward. An inventory session first lists the repository's usable rules. Family sessions then write up to 4 families each, 12 at most. A top-up session continues the numbering.
- **Screen in waves.** Families 1 to 8 are screened first, under the keep rule of prereg line 68. Families 9 to 12 are screened only for a repository with no admissible split after wave 1. A void screen session counts as missing. If a family's recorded sessions already fix its verdict, the verdict stands, as for hp-f4 in the stage 2 record; otherwise the family is screened again.
- **Set N.** Merged against every kept family of the repository. Set R's sequence takes the first 6 in merge order.
- **Pools.** A pool is 6 kept families. Pools are tried in lexicographic order of their sorted family numbers, so the first is the 6 lowest-numbered kept families.
- **Splits.** Within a pool, 3 families go to set X and 3 to set R. Splits are tried in lexicographic order of set X's sorted numbers. The first admissible split is taken.
- **Admissible.** Reversal families in set X and set R differ by at most 1. Each set holds at most 1 template family, so across the 10 repositories each set is at least two thirds maintainer rules (prereg line 51). Set X's lesson tasks draw an order on seeds 1 to 9 as sequence `<key>-x`. Set R's lesson tasks plus its 6 set N tasks draw an order on seeds 1 to 9 as sequence `<key>`. These are the sequence ids the scored spec uses.
- **No admissible split.** Wave 2 is screened. If there is still none, or fewer than 6 families are kept, a top-up session writes up to 4 more families from unused inventory rules; they are screened and the rule runs again. If the repository still has no admissible split, the next spare replaces it.

`C:/z0-runs/scored/split.mjs` (sha256 `9c4da180d4cfca570475084d509fc0ba9c8206818ca24371b4f12d8bd74c8160`) applies the rule to one repository's merged spec and kept list. It draws orders with the runner commit's `drawOrder` (`45b57c6b`, `scripts/token-eval/lessons.mjs`).

## Authoring

Authors run `scripts/token-eval/z0-author.mjs` from the runner commit (`45b57c6b`) on the subscription, with model `claude-opus-5-5`. Each author's checkout is built in place by `buildSnapshot` from the pinned source, so its git config and reflog name no outside path, and it must equal the gated snapshot commit.

- **Inventory.** One session per repository judges every rule line against criteria 1 to 6 and writes the kept list in its own order, the real rule changes found in `rules/history/`, and the template fits. Brief: `C:/eval-authors/s3x-inventory-template.md` (sha256 `28bf1f5090fd7cace68677cd5f866ae333ffac974451c9061dd1e104c8a5548d`; see the leak below for the version before, `8a6acd24`).
- **Family sessions.** Session `s` takes kept rules `4(s-1)` to `4s - 1` and the `s`-th fitting template, and numbers its families from `4s - 3`. Sessions 1 and 2 run in parallel. A rule is skipped as a duplicate when a lower-numbered kept rule asks for the same thing; the templates of sessions 1 and 2 count as number 0. Brief: `C:/eval-authors/s3x-family-template.md` (sha256 `766864e17684a33313f52f997b495f6a9ae762f0d3e6d69adafedb09bd464e23`; the version before was `0a73c64c`), filled by `C:/eval-authors/s3x-fill.mjs` (sha256 `d2fabf1040a58d11cda5e8dbc4c3d3a6f101997f49965db389dbb20b8c363514`) from `C:/eval-authors/s3x-repos.json`. The version before, `8d53a885`, had no rerun letter: a letter gives a stopped session a fresh directory, and zotero's session 2 reran as `s3x-zf-2r` because a leftover process still held a file open under the old one. The version before that, `0cb19834`, also refused the em dashes inside quoted maintainer rules (leakcanary's AGENTS.md has them). Now only the brief's own prose is checked, so the earlier briefs fill to the same text.
- **Author environment.** `C:/eval-authors/s3x-launch.ps1` (sha256 `9250efbb755a0e03286ff68562bf08f9f541d042a4ef2c46dd331df675f7c3a9`) starts each session with its toolchain's PATH and variables, the same toolchains and caches the test gate used. A Python author gets a virtual environment of its own beside its checkout, with the package installed from that checkout: the gate's environment points its editable install at a path outside, which the forbid scan would catch.
- **A leak through the process list, and the fix.** Two zotero family sessions were discarded by the forbid scan: session 1 at the end, session 2 at request 130, when it was stopped. Both had run `ps -ef` in Git Bash to find a hung command, and the list showed other sessions' command lines, which name hippo paths. The brief's working-directory paragraph now also says not to list or inspect processes, and to stop a hung command with the tool's timeout. Both briefs changed only in that line. Leakcanary's session 1 had started on the old brief 20 minutes before; it was stopped and restarted on the new one, and so were both zotero sessions. Sessions on the old briefs with a clean scan stand, since the scan is the test: the magnetissimo inventory and session 2, the zotero, leakcanary and rustfs inventories, and magnetissimo's session 1, which was well along and clean when the leak was found. Each author's request log is also scanned for the tool call behind the first hit (a script in the session scratchpad), so a discard names its source.
- **Snapshot half of the test gate.** `C:/z0-runs/scored/snapshot-gate.mjs` (sha256 `1bfc913ffc9c556c3eebe221b6debd9459f7db895e8e2212f5060b8473059506`) builds each snapshot from a blobless clone and reruns the upstream install and test commands on it. A snapshot whose tests fail only where upstream fails (same failing tests) gets the upstream pass and fail counts. The earlier version, `61b557d8`, opened each log append-only, and an append-only handle drops every write from an msys program. So linguist's `make` (run by rake) stopped with status 2 and printed nothing. Only the log handle changed. Linguist was the one repository whose result changed: rerun, it has 2,015 passes and 34 failures in 2,049 runs, against 2,013 and 35 upstream. The version after it, `bc73c696`, ran a Python snapshot in upstream's virtual environment. That environment's Scripts folder is first on PATH, so the install's `python -m venv` tried to overwrite the running interpreter (Errno 13), and the editable install would have pointed at the upstream clone. The current version gives each snapshot its own environment (`<name>-snap`); maigret and langextract were rerun on it, and nothing else changed.
- **A known bias of the frozen scrub.** `eval-snapshot.mjs` replaces every forbidden-term match in a text file, including a "z0" inside a base64 integrity hash in a lockfile. The install then fails the integrity check, and the snapshot fails the gate. Five of the seven JavaScript lockfiles gated so far held such a hash (zotero's and hackathon-starter's did not), and those five repositories dropped: ant-design-pro (npm), Folo, pnpm, nolebase and MetaEdit (pnpm). The scrub is part of the frozen runner, so the drops stand under the rule. The scored set therefore under-represents large JavaScript monorepos.
- **Timing under load, a rule set before any rerun.** Two snapshots failed the gate only on time, while about ten author sessions loaded the machine. Halo has upstream's one failing test in 2,159, and took 308.7 s against 246.3 s upstream. Langextract has upstream's 3 failures with 967 passes, and took 495.5 s against 253 s upstream. Each gets exactly one more timed run of the snapshot, started when no author session is running, and that run stands whatever it shows; both runs are recorded. Both come before hackathon-starter in tier 3, so these reruns decide the 10th scored repository and the spares. Halo, langextract and hackathon-starter are authored meanwhile, so the result does not wait on authoring.
- **The walk so far.** With the current gate files, `pick.mjs` picks 10 and no spares: linguist, zotero-format-metadata, rustfs, leakcanary, conductor, nacos, Turing.jl, maigret and magnetissimo in tier 1, then hackathon-starter in tier 3. Nacos's listed test command builds with its tests skipped and exits 0, so it passes as written; its authors are told which modules' tests pass and to check any other module at base. Conductor's and maigret's snapshots fail only upstream's tests (6 `DocumentAccessPolicyTest` failures; 4 failures and the `os.geteuid` import error). Author entries in `C:/eval-authors/s3x-repos.json` (sha256 `0e7278ae911234499b9eb9edc0b5395867049ab6fb02a54c2ebb728c20459447`) cover these 10 plus halo and langextract.
- **Langextract cannot be scored.** Its inventory kept 3 rules and 1 template fits, so it can never reach a pool of 6 families. Only halo's rerun can still add a repository.
- **Toolchains.** Repositories that ship only the Unix `gradlew` get a `gradlew.bat` shim on PATH (`C:/eval-envs/gradle-shim`), and Rust sessions get their own `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`. `C:/eval-authors/s3x-env.ps1` is now sha256 `17c012f0ae8ec1fe`; the version before, `11c537ee26c2286e`, set the same variables, and the screen launcher now reads the table too. The launcher (`65586e675baff118`) adds `-Round` for repair rounds.
- **Proofs.** Each finished session is proved by `C:/z0-runs/scored/prove.mjs` (sha256 `8cc0db5f710aef3e`), started by `C:/eval-authors/s3x-prove.ps1` (`29ca5a22d0aba9a1`). It runs each task the way the runner will: a fresh clone, the task's `setup`, the hidden test at base, fix, good and bad, the checkers, and the runner's own preflights. The first version let ignored output made by one side reach the next, so magnetissimo's bad side met the good side's migration in a test database. Now the good and bad sides each start from the state right after `setup`. Proofs run before that fix: mg-1, lc-1, li-2, zf-1 and rf-2.
- **Template slots.** Briefs filled before the reversal fix in `s3x-fill.mjs` showed only the first lesson's template slots, so authors named a reversal's slots themselves. `C:/z0-runs/scored/fix-template-slots.mjs` (`0d9024d449f378d1`) swaps in the inventory's reversal slots only when they rebuild the lesson's rule exactly. It changed lc-f2-l2, zf-f3-l2 and tu-f3-l2, and no other lesson.
- **Key phrases already in the code.** The runner refuses any task whose stub tree holds a lesson's key phrase. Authors had picked phrases that already run through the checkout, such as a module name or a CI command: 20 lessons in 8 of the 10 sessions finished by 15:30. The family brief now says to check every base with `git grep -i -F` (sha256 `6e49944d923c4028`; linguist sessions 3 and 4 used it).
- **Repair rounds.** A session whose proof fails gets one round on the same checkout: `C:/eval-authors/s3x-repair.mjs` (`878ec3ae9c0d6581`) writes the proof's problems, with outside paths scrubbed, and a brief from `s3x-repair-template.md` (`b0483bc57171dfbc`; the version before, `76ab0f188a776263`, had no checker timeout label). The author keeps every task and family id, replaces a task it cannot fix, and removes a family that cannot be saved. It never adds a family. Linguist's li-f5 was removed this way: its whole command sits in the CI config of every base.
- **Checker time.** Zotero's zf-f2 checker started about 80 git processes, and on the loaded machine it passed the runner's 120-second limit. The family brief (sha256 `7e80d31cc3ab9c7e`, from session 3 on) and the repair brief now ask for few processes.
- **Linguist and Turing.jl drop out.** Linguist kept 16 rules. Sessions 3 and 4 found every one of theirs a duplicate of a lower-numbered rule, session 1 qualified 2, and li-f5 was removed, so linguist can reach 2 families at most. Turing.jl kept 5 rules and 1 template, gave 4 families and has no unused rule left. Under the split rule each needs a spare, and the walk found none. Hackathon-starter kept 4 rules and 2 templates and gave 3 families; halo kept 6 rules and 1 template and gave 5. Neither has an unused rule left. So at most 7 repositories remain: zotero, rustfs, leakcanary, conductor, nacos, maigret and magnetissimo. Under the fewer-than-10 rule this was reported to Keith on 2026-10-16. Authoring, proofs and set N continue for the other repositories, since both ways forward need them.
- **Session 3.** Conductor, maigret, leakcanary, zotero and nacos started session 3 (kept rules 8 to 11) at 16:44 to 16:51 on brief `7e80d31cc3ab9c7e`. Their families are screened only if wave 1 leaves no admissible split. Zotero's session 3 found both its rules duplicates of lower-numbered rules and made no family, so zotero has 6.

## Convention pairs, a rule set before any pair was read

Set on 2026-10-16. The stage 2 record (S7, a leak voids Comonicon.jl's seed 1) gave the scored set a rule: two families from one repository may not come from one convention, and no key phrase may be the name of a convention that another family's rule belongs to. The stage 3 family briefs never got that line; their duplicate rule (a lower-numbered rule that asks for the same thing) is narrower. So before the screen, a reader who has seen no screen result reads each same-repository pair of families from their rules, source lines and key phrases.

- **One convention.** If two families' rules are parts of one convention, such as two lines of one commit-message standard, the higher-numbered family leaves, as a duplicate does.
- **A key phrase that names a convention.** If a lesson's key phrase names a convention that another family's rule belongs to, the lesson takes a new key phrase by the same test as a repair round: the shortest phrase that carries the rule's specific content and appears in no task's base, no prompt, and no other lesson's rule or reason. If none exists, the family leaves.
- A family that leaves counts as not kept for the split rule.
- **The first reading.** One Opus reader judged 145 pairs from sessions 1 and 2 of every repository and session 3 of leakcanary, nacos and magnetissimo; conductor's and maigret's session 3 were still writing. Its input is written by `C:/z0-runs/scored/pairs/pairs-input.mjs` (sha256 `74d6cc72153101ec`), and its verdict is `pairs-verdict.json` (`0685bc78c71406f1`) beside it. It flagged 7 pairs and named 16 close calls that it kept. Conductor and magnetissimo have no flag.
  - One convention, so the higher-numbered family leaves: rf-f2 (rustfs AGENTS.md lines 79 to 82, the docs-only verification tier, with rf-f1), rf-f6 (CONTRIBUTING.md lines 15 to 25, `cargo fmt`, with rf-f3), mt-f7 (CONTRIBUTING.md lines 60 and 116 to 128, with mt-f2) and mt-f4 (CONTRIBUTING.md lines 24 to 29, the hook that regenerates `db_meta.json`, with mt-f1).
  - A key phrase that names another family's convention: zf-f6-l1 (zf-f2's Fluent locale files), lc-f2-l2 (lc-f7's shark API file) and na-f2-l1 (na-f5's Spotless line). Each takes a new key phrase in a repair round. `s3x-repair.mjs` (now sha256 `2a300ff7a1a518dc`) takes the flags from `C:/z0-runs/scored/pair-flags.json` (`92f7e6fdf8b327ef`), and the repair brief (`a660b8ef473472d0`) gained one label for them. Zotero's session 2 and leakcanary's session 1 started those rounds; nacos's session 1 gets its flag with its proof's problems.
  - Rustfs falls to 5 families, so its session 3 (kept rules 8 to 11) started at 18:53 on the current family brief. Wave 2 is certain for it, as for leakcanary and nacos.
- **The second reading.** The same reader judged the 26 new pairs from conductor's and maigret's session 3 (21 and 5): input `pairs-input-2.json` (`b36cdbad5ca68955`), verdict `pairs-verdict-2.json` (`52750c2ce6e17de9`). It flagged none. Rustfs's session 3 gets the same reading once it is written.
- **Maigret.** Its author removed mt-f1 in mt-1's repair round. mt-f4 still leaves: it leaves as a duplicate does, and a duplicate is judged against the lower-numbered kept rule, whatever later happens to that rule's family. Maigret has 6 families: f2, f3, f5, f6, f8 and f9.
- **Key-phrase rounds.** Zotero's zf-f6-l1 and leakcanary's lc-f2-l2 took new key phrases from their authors. The combine step below checks every phrase against prompts, rules and stub trees again. Nacos's na-f2-l1 takes its flag in na-1's repair round.

## Set N rule, set before any set N task was ported or authored

Set on 2026-10-16. It follows stage 2 (`z0-stage2-record.md`, set N).

- **Ported first.** A porter turns upstream commits since 2025-01-01 into tasks on the snapshot: base is the snapshot with the commit's code change reverted, fix is the snapshot, and the hidden tests are the commit's test files. A candidate changes at most 4 runnable test files and 400 code lines. A task is kept only if its tests fail at base and pass at fix in a fresh clone, and the porter stops at 9 kept or 60 attempts. Its request is rewritten as a symptom by a blind author.
- **Authored to fill.** If fewer than 6 survive the merge, a blind author writes new tasks that break a tested behaviour on main, away from the files the families touch.
- **Merge.** Ported tasks come first, then authored ones, each in id order. A task is dropped if it changes a file that a family task or screen changes, or if its request holds any lesson's key phrase. Set R takes the first 6 (split rule).
- The porter is `C:/z0-runs/scored/setn/port-history.mjs`; its hash is recorded with the first ported tasks.
- **Porter hash.** `port-history.mjs` sha256 `cc3e1b94ddd3774b`, launched by `port.ps1` (`1a1d9c619825bac2`). Its last edit added a per-repo list of test files that already fail on the snapshot, which zotero's entry does not use. Zotero kept 7 tasks before its history ran out at 20 attempts; maigret kept 9 in 12 attempts. Repositories run two at a time.
- **Porter results.** Magnetissimo kept 2 in 41 attempts. Rustfs kept 0 in 60 attempts out of 1,604 candidates, so its set N is all authored. After the environment fix below, conductor kept 9 in 25 attempts, nacos 9 in 29 and leakcanary 3 in 60.
- **A porter environment fix.** Conductor's first porter run failed every candidate at base and fix: the porter passed the Claude Code shell's `NoDefaultCurrentDirectoryInExePath` to its test commands, so cmd.exe ran the PATH shim instead of conductor's own `gradlew.bat`, and the shim's `-jar` call does not fit conductor's older wrapper jar. The runner and `prove.mjs` already drop that variable. `port-history.mjs` (now sha256 `0987e81bc84d6ccd`) drops the same list. Conductor reran on it from the start; its first run is kept as `co.try1`. The repositories ported before (zotero, maigret, magnetissimo, rustfs) run no batch file from the checkout, so their results stand.
- **Set N authors.** `C:/eval-authors/s3x-setn-fill.mjs` (sha256 `d7bc4bf03e19e749`) builds each author checkout in place from the gated snapshot. It fetches only the porter's `task/*` branches, as one pack, and removes the fetch's reflogs and `FETCH_HEAD`. It freezes the porter's task list under `setn/frozen/` and writes the brief from `s3x-setn-template.md` (`27cee23caa00d782`). Part 1 asks for a symptom request for each ported task. Part 2 asks for new tasks that break a tested behaviour on `main`, and lists as off limits every file on `main` that a family task or screen changes. New-task counts: rustfs 8, magnetissimo 6, leakcanary 5, the other four 0. Each checkout passed the forbidden-term scan of its brief, task list and git metadata.

## Screen

- **Combine.** `C:/z0-runs/scored/combine.mjs` (sha256 `18224eaa8e836a51`) joins one repository's proved sessions into one spec on one run repo, leaves out families above the wave and those named by `--drop` (the convention-pair rule), and reruns the runner's schema, source, key-phrase and stub checks across all of them. Zotero's 6 families (f1 to f3 and f5 to f7, 20 tasks) passed all four.
- **Key phrases on main.** Leakcanary's three sessions (families f1, f2, f5, f6, f7 and f9, 20 tasks) passed schema, sources and phrases, but not stubs: lc-f9-l1's key phrase `Desktop.browse` sits on `main` in `OpenWithTheOs.kt`. lc-f9's own bases rewrite those lines, so its author's check of its own bases and its proof both passed, while every task base of the other sessions holds the phrase. The briefs told authors to check their own bases, never `main`. Three changes followed, none of them to a key phrase. The family brief (sha256 `669d8e697f430e46`) and the repair brief (`9c8a5e5b54b0c916`) now say to check `main` too. `s3x-repair.mjs` (`6790f3a5e5ff589c`) lists every lesson whose phrase is on `main`. `C:/z0-runs/scored/scan-stub-phrases.mjs` (`f13e8a2185d1b32a`) lists every stub hit in a combined repository, where the runner stops at the first. A scan of every session against `main` found such phrases in co-2, lc-3, mt-2, na-1, na-2, na-3 and rf-1. The co-2 and mt-2 repair prompts already listed theirs through their own bases, and were rewritten on the new brief as round 2. lc-3's author picks a new phrase in a repair round after the limit resets.
- **Launch.** `C:/z0-runs/scored/s3x-screen.ps1` (`412dca5813caf825`) runs `ab-run.mjs --screen` from the runner commit in the repository's toolchain environment and passes every toolchain variable through to the sessions. Pins are `C:/z0-runs/scored/pins.json` (`1ad132cceeaad345`): Claude Code 2.1.288, `claude-opus-5-5`, hippo `45b57c6b`. The out folder sits under `C:/z0-runs`, since the runner refuses one under a folder that holds a CLAUDE.md. Zotero's screen (36 sessions) started on 2026-10-16.
- **Weekly limit.** At about 19:17 on 2026-10-16 the subscription hit its weekly limit, which resets on 21 Oct at 02:00 London time. Since 14 Oct, author sessions had used 3,002 requests in 64 sessions. The screen's runner waits out a limit (15-minute polls, up to 8 days), so zotero's screen resumes by itself. An author session stops instead: rustfs's session 3 stopped after 23 requests and reruns under a new letter after the reset. Conductor's and maigret's session 2 repair rounds, started at 20:30, stopped after 2 requests each and rerun as round 2 with the same problems. Proofs and porters use no model and continue. Since then lc-3's and mt-3's proofs passed, and na-1's failed on key phrases in its stub trees; its repair prompt, with its convention-pair flag, waits for the reset.
- **Proofs and route after the limit.** Conductor's session 3 passed. Nacos 2 and rustfs 1 failed only on key phrases in their stub trees, which their repair prompts list. Magnetissimo's round 2 briefs were refilled from the current repair brief; none of its key phrases is on `main`. At 22:12 four repair rounds (leakcanary 3, conductor 2, maigret 2, nacos 1) started on the free Console credit through the swap proxy, which refuses every request once its own tally reaches $150. `s3x-launch.ps1 -Api` (`8eb9d4ab70942603`) runs them through `s3x-author-api.mjs` (`15ff684e7141258d`), the runner's `runAuthor` with that proxy as upstream, on the same model and brief, so the route changes no author input. The other sessions wait in `s3x-queue.ps1` (`d2743aeb71cfc7eb`), which probes the subscription every 5 minutes and, once a probe passes, launches four at a time.
- **Console results.** Every Console session finished with status 0: the repair rounds lc-3 r1, mt-2 r2, na-1 r1, co-2 r2, na-2 r1, rf-1 r1 and mg-1 to mg-3 r2, rustfs's session 3 rerun (`s3x-rf-3b`), and the set N authors of zotero, conductor and maigret. The proxy's tally stood at $142.12 over 3,749 requests at 23:27 on 2026-10-16, under its cap. Proofs passed for lc-3 r1, mt-2 r2, na-1 r1, na-2 r1, na-3, co-2 r2, mg-1 to mg-3 r2, rf-1 r1 and rf-3b.
- **A new subscription token.** The queue stopped twice by itself, and its probes never passed. Claude Code's permission check blocked a route that would have run the authors on this session's own login, and those edits were reverted. Keith made a new subscription token at about 00:20 on 2026-10-17. Since then sessions launch directly through `s3x-launch.ps1`, without the queue. The set N authors of magnetissimo, rustfs and leakcanary ran on it. Zotero's screen was still waiting on the old token's limit: it was stopped, its folder kept as `zf-w1/screen-limit1`, and it restarted at 00:25 in a fresh folder, since the runner refuses an out folder that holds records.

## Round 2 (draft prereg `2026-10-18-z0-round2-prereg.md`)

- **The fixed sizer at 3 families a repository.** On the stage 2 calibration (cj, bm, ml and bm set X; 470 records), at a 15-point minimum effect, `C:/z0-runs/r2-size/sizes-fixed-e0.15-f3.json` gives H1 win power over 0.9 at the point estimates from 10 repositories up, and at the bounds 0.146 at 10, 0.18 at 15, 0.34 at 20, 0.51 at 25 and 0.68 at 30 (5,187 to 8,414 sessions, set X included). At the bounds the seed rule stops 80% of runs at 10 repositories and 23% at 30: H1's noise bound is 0.34 against a point of 0.14, from 2 repositories and 4 degrees of freedom. The level check failed at 10 repositories ("rule at bounds" 0.0356 against a ceiling of 0.0299, read on the 20% of runs that did not stop). The sizer runs again with the dev-round runs added (prereg item 8) before any repository count is fixed.
- **Run at the cap, never stop.** The stop is the seed rule's own loss: a run whose wave 1 asks for more than 9 seeds scores nothing, and the runs that go on are those whose wave 1 read a small spread, which is why the level check failed. `--on-miss cap` (sizer and seed rule) runs such a hypothesis at 9 seeds. Sized on cj and bm alone (round 2 has no set X), at the families a repository each search chose (2, except 3 for the stop rule at 10), H1 bounds power under the stop rule and the cap rule: 0.146 and 0.436 at 10 repositories, 0.34 and 0.78 at 20, 0.514 and 0.867 at 25, 0.68 and 0.90 at 30. Under the cap rule the level check holds at 25 ("rule at bounds" 0.0259 against 0.0272). Sessions at 25 repositories: 4,550 at the point estimates, 10,583 with every rule arm at 9 seeds. Mutation check: with the cap branch removed, both new tests fail.
- **The cap rule is the default; the level check at 100,000 simulations.** `bf6dfe6c` makes `cap` the default of both CLIs and adds a "rule at point estimates" cell to the level check. At 25 repositories of 2 families, seed 1, the cap rule's worst cell (the rule at the bounds) reads 0.0257 against a ceiling of 0.0260, and the stop rule's reads 0.0305 against 0.0263 with 39% of runs stopped (`C:/z0-runs/r2-size/sizes-rn-cap-l100k.json`, `sizes-rn-stop-25-l100k.json`).
- **Toolchains for batch 2.** `C:/eval-authors/s3x-env.ps1` (now sha256 `4ebc96a6b1367348`) adds `go` (go1.27.2 with `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, so a go.mod that asks for a newer Go fails instead of downloading one; MSYS2 ucrt64 gcc last on PATH for cgo) and `dotnet` (SDK 11.0.100 RC 1, the only .NET runtime on the box, with `DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD=Major`; a net8.0 app restored and ran). By the shortlist's toolchain field (only names, counts and toolchains of batch 2 were read), 44 of its 50 repositories map to an entry; 4 Dart, 1 Yarn and 1 PHP repository do not.
- **Dev round 1: c1 replaced by c1b before any judging.** Candidate c1 (`88c47f67`, branch `z0/r2-c1`) pins every statement of 4 or more words in a user turn after the session's first; its A2 check-half runs `co-c1`, `zf-c1` and `mg-c1` started at 12:52. A code review of c1 found three defects: a VS Code `--turn` slice read its first user turn as the session's request, Claude Code's `!` shell and task-notice lines counted as a person's words, and any reply of 4 words, a thanks too, became a pinned rule. Candidate c1b (`b89e6e8a`, branch `z0/r2-c1b`, pins `C:/z0-runs/dev/pins-c1b.json`) fixes all three: a follow-up is pinned only with a code token or a limiting word. On the capture probe c1b equals c1 on every file (dev 38 of 40, every rustfs teach message). c1b's runs `co-c1b`, `zf-c1b`, `mg-c1b` and tune-half `cj-c1b`, `bm-c1b` started at 14:11 and 14:16.
- **c1b fails the paraphrase check; c1c replaces it.** On the dev lessons the runner's two teach forms stored the key phrase 20 of 20 times and the reworded forms 18 of 20, so c1b fails prereg item 4 ("as often as"). The two misses are co-f9 and co-f10, whose rules are fragments of 2 and 3 words, under the 4-word floor when reworded form 2 states the rule alone. Candidate c1c (`8f0bd028`, branch `z0/r2-c1c`, pins `C:/z0-runs/dev/pins-c1c.json`) keeps a follow-up that names code from 2 words; a limiting word with no code still needs 4. The probe reads 40 of 40 on the dev lessons and every teach message on rustfs, cj, bm, hp and ml; on the runner's two teach forms c1c gives the same items as c1b for all 34 dev and tune-half messages. The c1b runs were stopped at 14:19 after 1 or 2 sessions each, before any grade was read, and stay unjudged. Disclosure: two c1b and c1c choices came from check-half lesson text (co-f9 for the bracket and call cue, co-f9 and co-f10 for the 2-word floor), so the probe is a tuned read on those lessons; their run outcomes were not read. The full suite ran on c1b, with one failure, a slow-git timing test that also failed on c1; on c1c the 22 test files that touch capture pass (320 tests). c1c's A2 runs `co-c1c`, `zf-c1c` (subscription 2), `mg-c1c` and tune-half `cj-c1c` (subscription 2), `bm-c1c` started at 14:21; the runner scripts are unchanged from `45b57c6b`. Round 1 judges c1c and reports c1's runs beside it; the tune-half runs are not judged and give round 2 c1c's chain if round 1 keeps it.
- **cj's leak, and the leak probe.** `cj-c1c` lost seeds 1 and 3 to G3: after cj-f3's teach (breaking changes), Claude Code's own memory held cj-f2's key phrase "Conventional commits", and hippo's copy of that memory held it too (source `agent-memory:claude-code`), so the void is the stage 2 S7 pair, not c1c's capture. cj is a stage 2 family and predates the convention-pair rule. So that no scored family can void this way, prereg item 7 gains a leak probe, `C:/z0-runs/scored/entangle.mjs`: each lesson's teach alone in A1 and in A2 on the build under test, on seeds 1 and 2, then G3's own scan for every other lesson's key phrase. `s3x-prescreen.ps1 -Kind entangle` launches it. The first version ran A1 alone, and on cj (`C:/z0-runs/dev/entangle-cj`, 6 sessions) it called every lesson clear. Over every session where cj-f3 was taught before cj-f2 (stage 2 calibration seed 1, `cj-c1c` seeds 1 and 3, and that probe), Claude Code's memory held cj-f2's key phrase after cj-f3's teach in 3 of 3 A2 sessions and in 0 of 5 A1, A4 and A5 sessions, so the leak comes from A2 and the probe now runs A2 too. Rerun on cj-f3 with c1c (`C:/z0-runs/dev/entangle-cj2`, 4 sessions), the probe calls cj-f3 entangled with cj-f2: the phrase was in 1 of 4 sessions, A1's seed 2. So both arms can leak, at a rate well under one, which is why the probe takes both arms and two seeds; an A2 session wrote its own memory note on both seeds, A1 on one of two. A probe hit is evidence of a leak; a clean probe only makes one less likely. The family brief also gained the convention-pair line the record says it lacked; its new sha256 is set at the tag.
- **c2, the round 2 candidate.** On the tune half, c1c pins a rule and its later reversal alike, and the prompt hook shows both with the same date (`C:/z0-runs/dev/reversal-probe.mjs` on bm-f5, hp-f4 and rf-f5), so after a reversal the agent sees two rules that disagree. Candidate c2 (`add23150`, branch `z0/r2-c2` on c1c, pins `C:/z0-runs/dev/pins-c2.json`) lets a person's later pinned rule supersede an earlier session's pinned rule from the same source when the two share a backtick code span or pass the conflict check's word overlap (Jaccard 0.5, at least 2 shared words); rules from one capture never supersede each other. On the tune half and rustfs, a shared code span joins only reversal pairs; word overlap alone misses rf-f5 (0.16). On `add23150` the reversal probe no longer shows the earlier rule after any of the three reversals; rf-f5's reversal is kept as its two sentences. Only tune-half and rustfs lesson text shaped c2. The full suite has one failure, a Slack smoke test that timed out at 5 s under suite load; its code path does not touch c2's files, and alone it passes in 3.5 s on c2. c2's A2 runs `co-c2`, `zf-c2` (subscription 2) and `mg-c2` started at 16:01, before round 1's decision, so that round 2 can be judged against whichever build round 1 keeps.
- **A runner stop, and its fix.** `zf-c2` stopped at 16:22 after 8 records with `ECONNRESET ... realpath '\\ Remove ...'`. The G1 read check resolves every path an agent's shell call names, and that token starts with two backslashes, so on Windows it is a `\\server\share` path and realpath asked the network; ECONNRESET is not on the list of path errors (`readcheck.mjs:156`), so the runner stopped. The same token folds without error at other times, so the stop depends on the network, not the run. `00ce7f4a` (branch `z0/runner-unc`, from `45b57c6b`) folds such a path as written, since it names another machine and never a run's dir; a test seam fakes the network error, and with the fix taken out the new test fails. The full read-check file passes (42 tests). `c050653b` (branch `z0/r2-c2r`) is c2 with that fix; its dist hash equals c2's (`0b2dd6a7`). `zf-c2r` (pins `C:/z0-runs/dev/pins-c2r.json`, subscription 2) started at 17:11 on all 3 seeds; `zf-c2`'s 8 records are not used.
- **The tune half leaves no link for a round 3.** Over the 27 apply sessions of `cj-c1c` and `bm-c1c` (69 valid records), c1c's A2 stored, showed and captured the lesson every time, and the agent followed it every time but once, in bm-f4 (5 of 6), after the rule was shown (`dev-report.mjs C:/z0-runs/dev/cj-c1c C:/z0-runs/dev/bm-c1c`). `bm-c1c` ended with 51 of 51 records, none void, and no failed hippo hook (slowest 1,679 ms). A round picks the weakest chain link on the tune half (round 2 prereg, item 3), so a round 3 runs only if a link opens there on the kept build. `co-c2` ended with 27 records, one voided by the G1 read check (the baselines have these too: 4 of 81 in `co-b0`), and no failed hook (slowest 956 ms).
- **Setup failures, and a retry.** Four of 211 zotero dev cells (`zf-b0`, `zf-c1b`, `zf-c1c`, `zf-c2r`, one each; two of them teach cells) were invalid on setup: `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` stopped with os error 183 or 5 on a folder in a `node_modules` the runner had just emptied. With no agent session at all, 100 clean installs of the zotero repository under the toolchain's pnpm 12.3.4 failed 3 times with os error 183 (`C:/z0-runs/dev/pnpm-race`), so the race is in pnpm, not in a process a session left. pnpm 12.4.1 lists no fix for it. Runner commit `4d3132e4` (branch `z0/runner-unc`) runs a failed setup once more from a fresh checkout and keeps the first try's log; a test fails once and then passes, and it fails with the retry removed. The tag carries it with `00ce7f4a`. `zf-c2r` ended with 33 records, the one setup cell invalid, and no failed hook (slowest 706 ms); `mg-c2` with 27, one G1 read void, and no failed hook (slowest 861 ms).
- **Round 1 keeps c1c.** `mg-b0` ended with 81 records (one A5 G1 read void, no failed hook; slowest 894 ms), so round 1 was judged at about 20:05 on the check half (co, zf, mg; 27 units, 340 valid records): `dev-report.mjs C:/z0-runs/dev/co-b0 C:/z0-runs/dev/zf-b0 C:/z0-runs/dev/mg-b0 --candidate C:/z0-runs/dev/co-c1c --candidate C:/z0-runs/dev/zf-c1c --candidate C:/z0-runs/dev/mg-c1c`. On repeat mistakes (candidate minus comparator, so lower is better) c1c's A2 against the b0 build's A2 reads -0.062 (SE 0.038) under both codings, more than one SE. Its gain over A5 grows: b0's A2 against A5 reads +0.056 (SE 0.031) and c1c's -0.006 (SE 0.035). The paraphrase check reads 40 of 40. So c1c is kept, and it is the best build. On the check half the chain moved where c1c aimed: co-f10's rule was stored but shown in 0 of 6 b0 A2 sessions and in 6 of 6 c1c ones, and mg-f4, mg-f5 and mg-f6 went to shown in every session. Disclosure: c1, replaced before judging, reads -0.074 (SE 0.035) against b0's A2 on the same runs.
- **Round 2 drops c2.** The best build's A2 is c1c's, so `swap-arm.mjs` put the c1c A2 records into each b0 run (`C:/z0-runs/dev/r2-base/co-b0c1c`, `zf-b0c1c`, `mg-b0c1c`; each reproduces c1c's round 1 numbers), and c2's runs (`co-c2`, `zf-c2r`, `mg-c2`) were paired with it. c2's A2 against c1c's reads -0.0123 with an SE of 0.0123 under both codings: one unit of 27 moved (-1/3), and with one nonzero unit the gain equals its SE exactly, so it is not larger than one SE. Its gain over A5 is -0.019 (SE 0.032) against c1c's -0.006, and its paraphrase check reads 40 of 40, but the first condition fails, so c2 is not kept.
- **No round 3; c1c is the frozen build.** Round 1 kept a change, so the two-round stop did not end the rounds. A round picks the weakest chain link on the tune half, and on the kept build the tune half has none (the bullet "The tune half leaves no link for a round 3"), so the rounds end and c1c goes to the tag. On the check half c1c's A2 against A1 reads -0.093 (SE 0.056), and against A5 -0.006 (SE 0.035): in 23 of 27 units the two arms made repeat mistakes at the same rate, so these dev families say little about capture. This is what criterion 7 and the A0 probe aim at in batch 2.
- **The teach-template ban, scoped.** At the tag check, `git diff z0-freeze z0/r2-c1c -- src/` matched "because" once, in a comment of `src/eval/repository-t.ts` (the analyzer's t test, from `573ddc8f`, which the pilot's runner `45b57c6b` already carried). No hippo source outside `src/eval/` imports that file or `eval-stats.ts`, and the analyzer hash pins both, so item 4 now leaves them out instead of a one-word comment edit that would have changed the dist hash the dev runs used. With them left out, c1c's diff holds no match.
- **The tag and the sizing.** `z0-r2-freeze` (`b713269e`, branch `z0/r2-freeze`) merges c1c, `z0/runner-unc` (`00ce7f4a`, `4d3132e4`) and this branch; its `src/` equals c1c's and its dist hash is c1c's (`b940a448`). On the tag tree the full suite passed (10,213 tests, 25 skipped) and so did the eval suite (161, 1 skipped). Pins: `C:/z0-runs/scored/pins-r2.json`. The sizer ran on `cal-cj4`, `cal-bm4` and the three check-half dev runs with c1c's A2, and chose 30 repositories of 2 families (`docs/evals/z0-r2-freeze/sizes.json`; the prereg, item 8, has the numbers). The dev runs moved the estimates: H1's noise bound fell from 0.343 to 0.061 and its family bound rose from 0.033 to 0.092, on 5 repositories instead of 2. The 657 sessions of the dev rounds and probes count in the 3,000-session authoring budget.

## Batch 2 pick and split rules, set before any batch 2 gate result was read

Set on 2026-10-18, after the tag `z0-r2-freeze` and the commit that fixed the round 2 prereg. No batch 2 repository had been cloned, installed or tested when this section was committed.

- **Input.** `C:/z0-runs/scored-shortlist/b2/candidates.json`, sha256 `bd4a0aa596446fd7742ee94e7ea6daaedd4d4d4222101266ba7c426efc9028d2`: 50 public repositories with their pin, language, licence, rule files, test command and checkable rule lines.
- **Eligible.** As in stage 3: an OSI licence, at least 4 checkable rule lines, and a product that is not agent instruction files.
- **Order.** Each eligible repository's sort key is the sha256 of `<candidates sha256>:<owner/name>`, ascending, as in stage 3. No rule count enters it.
- **Test gate.** The stage 3 gate, unchanged: the test command runs at the pin, once to warm the caches and once timed, and passes if the timed run takes 300 seconds or less and either exits 0 or has 20 or more passes with at most 2% failures. An upstream clone and the eval snapshot must both pass. A command that cannot run as written on Windows gets the smallest faithful translation, made by a test worker who never saw this rule, and `testgate.json` records it.
- **Walk.** Every eligible repository goes to the gate. Authoring takes gated repositories in tiers, each tier in the order above: tier 1 those with 6 or more rule lines, at most 3 of a language; tier 2 the rest with 6 or more; tier 3 those with 4 or 5. Authoring stops taking new repositories once 30 qualify (round 2 prereg, items 5 and 8) or the authoring budget ends.
- **Families.** As in stage 3, an inventory session lists the usable rules and family sessions write up to 4 families each, numbered in authoring order. Each family goes to the A0 probe, then, if kept, to the leak probe, then to the screen (round 2 prereg, item 7).
- **Split.** Round 2 has set R and set N only. A repository qualifies with at least the design's families a repository among its screen-kept families. Its set R takes its lowest-numbered kept families up to the design's count, with at most 1 template family, so the set stays at least two thirds maintainer rules (prereg line 51). Set N is merged against every kept family, and set R's sequence takes the first 6 set N tasks in merge order. The sequence id is the repository key, with orders drawn on seeds 1 to 9 by the runner's `drawOrder`.
- **Fewer than the design.** If fewer repositories qualify than the design needs, the sizer runs again at the count reached (round 2 prereg, item 8).
- **One exclusion, 2026-10-18.** 30 candidates have 4 or more rule lines and an OSI licence. `virgiliojr94/book-to-skill` is out: the repository is itself a Claude Code skill (`SKILL.md` at the root), so its product is agent instruction files. The call came from its GitHub description and top-level file list, after its gate worker started and before any gate result for it was read. 29 repositories go to the gate. The pick script is `C:/z0-runs/scored/b2/pick.mjs`.

## Batch 3, set before any batch 3 gate result was read

Set on 2026-10-18. Batch 2 has 29 eligible repositories, so it cannot reach 30 qualifying alone. Batch 3 was screened while the batch 2 gate ran, and no batch 3 repository had been cloned, installed or tested when this section was committed.

- **Shortlist.** `C:/z0-runs/scored-shortlist/build_b3.py` runs batch 2's checks and continues the same interleaved pool order after the 415 repositories that batches 1 and 2 screened. It differs in two ways. It drops Dart, PHP and Scala repositories, because no toolchain for them is installed and the gate brief bars installing one. It keeps only repositories with 4 or more checkable rule lines, and it stops at 50 of those (442 screened).
- **Input.** `C:/z0-runs/scored-shortlist/b3/candidates.json`, sha256 `a5bc6b2a581756aefee233469c1c9d0c0731567d6817efc1b84d3edbb06a864b`.
- **Rules.** Batch 2's rules above, unchanged, with this file's sha256 in the sort key. The licence set adds `LGPL-2.1` and `EUPL-1.2`, both OSI-approved. `simple-icons/simple-icons` (`CC0-1.0`, not OSI-approved) is out.
- **Two exclusions.** `davila7/claude-code-templates` (its product is Claude Code agent, command and settings templates) and `Yeachan-Heo/oh-my-codex` (agent hooks, prompts and skills, like `oh-my-claudecode` in stage 3). The calls came from each repository's GitHub description and top-level file list.
- **Walk.** 47 repositories go to the gate (`C:/z0-runs/scored/b3/queue.json`, `C:/z0-runs/scored/b3/pick.mjs`). Batch 3's authoring order follows all of batch 2's: tiers run within each batch, and the stop at 30 qualifying counts both batches.

## Batch 4, set before any batch 4 gate result was read

Set on 2026-10-20 at 08:40. Batches 2 and 3 gated 22 repositories, so round 2 cannot reach 30 qualifying (below, "Too few repositories for the design"), and Keith chose a batch 4. No batch 4 repository had been cloned, installed or tested when this section was committed.

- **Shortlist.** `C:/z0-runs/scored-shortlist/build_b4.py` (sha256 `56c1780486a983cc`) runs batch 3's checks and continues the same interleaved pool order after the 857 repositories that batches 1 to 3 screened. It stops at 80 kept repositories, since batches 2 and 3 gated 8 of 29 and 14 of 47. Its replay check asserts that batch 3's 442 come first.
- **Input.** `C:/z0-runs/scored-shortlist/b4/candidates.json` as the script leaves it when it stops. Its sha256 goes into the sort key, and into this record when the screen ends.
- **Rules.** Batch 3's rules, unchanged: the licence set of `b3/pick.mjs`, at least 4 checkable rule lines, and no product that is agent instruction files, judged from each repository's GitHub description and top-level file list before any gate result for it is read.
- **An early gate.** Every eligible repository goes to the gate whatever its place in the order, so the gate takes each candidate as the screen keeps it. `C:/z0-runs/scored/b4/queue.json` holds them in the order the screen kept them. The order by sort key decides only the tiers.
- **Rust.** A rust repository's gate spec uses `rust-ucrt` from `s3x-env.ps1`, the PATH that every batch 2 and 3 rust gate used.
- **Walk.** Tiers run within the batch, at most 3 of a language in tier 1, and batch 4's authoring order follows batch 3's. The stop at 30 qualifying counts all three batches.

## Round 2 authoring tools

- **Fill.** `C:/eval-authors/r2-fill.mjs` is `s3x-fill.mjs` with `r2-` session folders, its own `C:/eval-authors/r2-repos.json`, and the round 2 inventory brief (prereg item 6). The family brief is unchanged. `s3x-fill.mjs` stays as it was, so the stage 3 briefs still fill to the same text.
- **Early start.** Authoring starts on a repository once its tier 1 place is certain: it has 6 or more rule lines, it passed both gates, and fewer than 3 repositories of its language come before it in the order. The order then only decides which repositories fall past the stop at 30, and that stop is many places away. Quarkdown (Kotlin) and github-mcp-server (Go) started this way at 21:18.
- **JDK 17.** Quarkdown pins Kotlin's `jvmTarget` to 17, which fails under JDK 21, so `C:/eval-authors/s3x-env.ps1` gains a `gradle17` toolchain with the box's JDK 17 (sha256 now `0016116bce7fb502`). The gate worker used the same JDK.
- **Two subscriptions.** `s3x-launch.ps1` and `C:/z0-runs/scored/s3x-screen.ps1` take `-TokenVar`, as `s3x-run.ps1` does. The screen script also runs the box check first, and takes the pinned Claude Code copy (`C:/z0-runs/scored/bin/claude.exe`, 2.1.288) and a `-Runner` folder. The stage 3 screens ran `C:/Users/skf_s/.local/bin/claude.exe`, which auto-updated past the pin after them.
- **A z0 path and three secrets in every child's env, and the fix.** Session `r2-gm-1` was discarded by the forbid scan after 10 requests. It printed PATH, and the User PATH holds a dead entry, `C:/z0-runs/scored/dotnet-home\.dotnet\tools`. Its source is not checked; the .NET SDK adds such a tools folder to the User PATH on its first run. The check also showed that every launcher's children inherited the User variables `Z0_SUB_TOKEN`, `Z0_SUB_TOKEN_2` and `Z0_CONSOLE_KEY`, because the runner's `armEnv` strips only its own prefixes (`arms.mjs:19-20`). None of the 109 author verdicts so far names a `Z0_` variable or a key prefix; `r2-gm-1` is the only PATH hit. `Set-S3xEnv` in `C:/eval-authors/s3x-env.ps1` (now sha256 `08c179ade26d3b85`), which every launcher calls, now removes every `Z0_*` variable and every PATH entry that matches the forbid pattern before a child starts. Launchers still read the token from User scope. `r2-qd-1`, `r2-qd-2` and `r2-gm-2` started before the fix; `r2-gm-1b` reruns gm session 1.
- **pnpm 12 and unapproved build scripts.** The eslint plugin's install exits 1 under pnpm 12.3.4 (`ERR_PNPM_IGNORED_BUILDS`, esbuild) upstream and on the snapshot, although the install is complete and all 430 tests pass. Its authors are told to install with `--config.dangerously-allow-all-builds=true`, which exits 0 and leaves the tree clean.

## Gate and authoring after the weekly limits, 2026-10-19

- **Weekly limits.** Both subscriptions reached their weekly limits during the first family sessions. `r2-qd-1`, `r2-qd-2`, `r2-gm-1b`, `r2-er-1` and `r2-er-2` ended at status 1 on the limit message, so they are void and rerun with a rerun letter (`r2-qd-1b`, `r2-qd-2b`, `r2-gm-1c`, `r2-er-1b`, `r2-er-2b`; each brief equals the cut one apart from its folder). `Z0_SUB_TOKEN`'s message names 05:00, which is 20 Oct; `Z0_SUB_TOKEN_2`'s names 24 Oct, 05:00. The same limit on this account's Sonnet sub-agents stopped the three gate workers. `r2-gm-2` finished first: its proof passed (`C:/z0-runs/scored/proofs/r2-gm-2`) with one family, the template family gm-f5, since its 4 maintainer rules failed criterion 3 or repeated earlier ones.
- **The gate goes on with Opus workers.** From 19:28 three Opus workers run the rest of the gate (batch 2 indices 19 to 30, then batch 3) from `C:/z0-runs/scored/gate2-brief.md` (sha256 `fd51fb159ae2d132`). The brief writes down the toolchain calls the first workers made one by one: Dart/Flutter, PHP, Yarn (as for lighthouse), Bun, Bazel, Docker and running services count as toolchain missing; poetry or uv may be pip-installed into a bootstrap venv; with no `make`, the target's commands run directly; a placeholder in a test command becomes one core module; Gradle runs with `--no-build-cache` and a clean-test task; pnpm gets the ignored-builds flag above. Up to three workers and one snapshot gate run at once. Load only slows a run, so it cannot pass a repository that a quiet box would fail; a failure on time alone gets the "Timing under load" rerun.
- **flutter_server_box.** Its test command (`make test-one`) runs `flutter test`, so it is recorded as toolchain missing. The first worker built only its Rust half, which does not count as the repository's test.
- **Rule sources.** The shortlist matched rule file names without regard to case, but eval-snapshot's names are exact (`CONTRIBUTING.md`). Poetry's 4 rule lines sit in `docs/contributing.md`, which the snapshot keeps as an ordinary file, so its rules would stay in every arm's checkout. Both pick scripts now count a rule line only if eval-snapshot copied its file into `rules/`, and drop a repository with fewer than 4 (`b2/pick.mjs` sha256 `aed5aa0ed65c31c0`, `b3/pick.mjs` `6937a284cd8a4d22`). Poetry is out on this; no other repository with a snapshot loses a rule line.
- **Snapshot venvs.** The gate brief puts a Python venv beside the clone (`<slug>-venv`), but `snapshot-gate.mjs` gave the snapshot its own venv only for stage 3's `eval-envs/venvs` paths, so poetry's snapshot install went into its upstream venv. Both batches' copies now also rename `-venv` and `-boot` folders under `clones/` (`b2` sha256 `ca9d404fcedd918c`, `b3` `38f46608c63363a1`). No other Python repository has reached its snapshot gate.
- **ktor came from the build cache.** Its timed upstream run and both snapshot runs took Gradle's build cache (`jvmTest FROM-CACHE`), so no test ran in them. Both reran at about 19:33 with `--no-build-cache`. Upstream: 172 passed, 0 failed, 8 skipped, 16.0 s. Snapshot: 172 passed, 1 failed, 8 skipped, 17.2 s, exit 0; the failure is `FileChannelTest`, which also failed in the first upstream run, so its authors are told to keep clear of it. Quarkdown's runs had no cache hit. ktor is tier 1 (Kotlin, 23 rule lines, the second Kotlin repository in the order), so it joins `r2-repos.json` as `kt` (sha256 now `c96cfd86a9cb0980`).
- **Batch 3 snapshot counts.** googleworkspace/cli: exit 101, 699 passed, 2 failed (the same ADC tests as upstream). highlight.js: exit 2, 601 passed, 2 failed, because the forbid scrub changed G-code markup fixtures that hold `Z0` coordinates. Both pass on counts.
- **One repository map.** `combine.mjs`, `merge-setn.mjs`, `s3x-repair.mjs`, `s3x-setn-fill.mjs` and `s3x-prove.ps1` read only the stage 3 map, so none could find a round 2 repository. `C:/eval-authors/repo-config.mjs` (sha256 `9c35a3f4f2747d98`) reads that map and `r2-repos.json` as one key space and stops on a key found in both; all five use it now (`combine.mjs` `e037babfb9d5c110`, `merge-setn.mjs` `af1a40f5069e4780`, `s3x-repair.mjs` `8f4aaa47e44e7e27`, `s3x-setn-fill.mjs` `43fed251d5e51822`, `s3x-prove.ps1` `41b731f96065e139`). The stage 3 map keeps its pinned sha256. `C:/z0-runs/scored/origins.json` gains the four round 2 source clones.

The cause is not checked; a Defender change would be Keith's call. A rebuild gives the same snapshot: `r2-fill.mjs` stops if the commit differs from the gated one.
- **Go test counts.** `go test` without `-v` prints one line per package, so the first workers counted packages. Among the 22 drops: grape fails its snapshot because `rake` runs rubocop first and `Layout/EndOfLine` flags every LF file; zeroclaw's snapshot removes an `AGENTS.md` that a crate compiles in; typeorm's fourth install, with the brief's allow-all-builds flag, fails in the libpq native build; kestra and floci need JDK 25; thunderbird-android needs the Android SDK; atuin's install fails on a git symlink; QwenPaw's pytest collection stops on two test modules with one name; sure needs PostgreSQL; jj has 43 snapshot-test mismatches. rubocop (312.4 s) and Vibe-Trading (475.7 s) upstream, and go-zero's snapshot (670.3 s, a hang in `core/queue`), wait for their one quiet run (`C:/z0-runs/scored/quiet-reruns.txt`).

Four more wait for a quiet run: agentscope (302.7 s, every test passed), mongoose (459.1 s), Lux.jl (900 s timeout) and ScrapeGraphAI, whose gate never finished because its worker's own 590 s shell limit killed the warm run. The records for dapr and picoclaw are not complete yet.
- **Early start for every gated repository.** The authoring budget is 3,000 sessions and 667 are spent, so the budget cannot end the walk. Batch 2's tier 3 repositories come before every batch 3 repository in the order, so faker and aurora-rss-reader start with batch 3's tier 1. `r2-repos.json` (sha256 now `139a51ecf82ea5ce`) gains `k6`, `um`, `gw`, `hl`, `hu`, `jv`, `fk`, `au` and `pg`, and `origins.json` (`013b5054782d31e6`) their source clones. A quiet run's time and exit replace the timed run's; a non-zero quiet run counts only once its pass and fail counts are added from its log. `b2/pick.mjs` is now sha256 `6d8c533d090b07b0`, `b3/pick.mjs` `650b9a40d2ce2611`.
- **The quiet runs.** `C:/z0-runs/scored/quiet-rerun.py` (sha256 `7519eb6b2309e606`) gives one gate failure on time alone its one more timed run, with the gate's environment, and records `quietSeconds`, `quietExit` and `quietLog` beside the first runs. `quiet-all.sh` (`2a5f6e98f16f9697`) runs the ten in `quiet-reruns.txt` one at a time, from 22:50 on 19 Oct, when no author session runs. The snapshot lane was stopped after pulumi's snapshot, so a repository that passes its quiet run does not start a snapshot gate during the others. ScrapeGraphAI gets the gate's full test phase (`run.py 27 test`, both runs). Its worker's 590 s shell limit stopped `run.py` but left a pytest process, stalled in `tests/test_chromium.py` since 20:26; it is not chased.
- **Go counts and cgo.** `go-count.sh` (sha256 now `d6b035d0bb3b647d`) keeps the caller's `CGO_ENABLED` and sets 1 only when it is unset, so a count runs the same build as the test command it counts. It also resolves its output path before it changes to the test folder: the first go-zero and pulumi snapshot counts got relative paths and stopped before any test ran.

Seven fail on time again and drop: rubocop 437.1 s, Vibe-Trading 668.6 s, agentscope 323.4 s (every test passed), biome 349.3 s, dapr 311.1 s, and Lux.jl and cognee at the 900 s cap. ScrapeGraphAI's first complete gate run timed out at 900 s in both runs, so it drops on time too. go-zero's snapshot took 109.6 s and exits 1 in `core/logx` and `core/proc`; `go-count.sh` counts 3,597 passed and 11 failed, and its 7 failing top-level tests are upstream's 7, so it passes on counts (tier 3, Go, 4 rule lines). mongoose upstream took 253.1 s with 4,667 passing, 47 pending and 1 failing (a mocha timeout), so it passes on counts. - **mongoose's snapshot.** The lane ran it alone after the quiet runs: 94.4 s, exit 1, with 4,667 passing, 47 pending and the same 1 failing test, so it passes on counts (tier 3, JavaScript, 5 rule lines). The lane was stopped again after it.
- **Gate result after the quiet runs.** 22 repositories are gated: batch 2 adds go-zero to its 7, and batch 3 adds pulumi and mongoose to its 12. `r2-repos.json` (sha256 now `e201d36014ffc5bb`) gains `gz`, `pm` and `mo`, and `origins.json` (`5e549ec061e1c026`) their source clones. Each single-test command ran once from a fresh snapshot clone, and each inventory brief is filled. `C:/z0-runs/scored/snap-counts.sh` (`f5f4e3b72b42deef`) ran the go-zero and pulumi counts. mongoose's tests start mongod with mongodb-memory-server, which downloads it when no copy is found. - **Wave 1, 20 Oct.** `r2-kt-inv` started at 05:07 on `Z0_SUB_TOKEN` and showed no limit message after 3 minutes, so at 05:10 `r2-qd-1b`, `r2-qd-2b`, `r2-gm-1c`, `r2-er-1b`, `r2-er-2b`, `r2-k6-inv` and `r2-um-inv` started. `r2-um-inv` finished first, at 05:15, with status 0 and a clean scan: 10 kept rules and one fitting template (`test-flag`). Its family sessions 1 and 2 started at 05:18. `r2-um-2` ended at 05:19 with no family: its four rules were three duplicates of the lower-numbered n5 and one test command the checkout already shows (criterion 3). That leaves unity-mcp at most 4 families from session 1, so its session 3 (kept rules 9 and 10) started at 05:24, beside sessions 1 and 2 of ktor (26 kept, three templates fit) and k6 (8 kept, `test-flag` fits), whose inventories ended clean. Sessions 1 and 3 also made no family. Session 1 found every rule's key phrase already on `main` (the `test-flag` phrase `uv run pytest` is in 8 files, rule 5's sentence is quoted in `Server/tests/test_tool_test_symmetry.py`), and session 3's two rules were duplicates. No kept rule is left, so unity-mcp has no family and cannot qualify. Its 4 sessions count against the budget. Subscription 1 showed no limit message, so the next three inventories in map order (`gw`, `hl`, `hu`) started at 05:26 inside the cap of 12 running authors, ahead of the 06:47 launch. By 05:41 the inventories of highlight.js (8 kept), javalin (3), googleworkspace/cli (19), heroui (18) and faker (12) had ended clean, and highlight.js's and javalin's sessions 1 and 2 started at 05:37. `r2-gm-1c` (2 families) passed its proof: every task fails on base and passes on fix. Its `prove.log` is empty and its process was gone, so the process stopped after it wrote `proof.json` (`prove.mjs:175`) and before it printed the summary; `proof.json` holds the result. `r2-er-1b` and `r2-er-2b` (3 families each) are being proved. At 05:41 memory held the next launches: 8.6 GB available, with Java builds at 31.7 GB private. `r2-er-1b` passed. `r2-er-2b` failed on `er-f6-screen` alone: one hidden test took 11.0 s against vitest's 5 s test timeout at fix, good and bad, while the box paged, and the file's other two tests passed. A hidden test that times out under load would also be noisy in the screen and the arms, so it gets a repair round (`repair-1.md`, from `s3x-repair.mjs`), not a second proof run. By 06:47 eight family sessions had ended clean: highlight.js 1 and 2 (3 families each), javalin 1 (3) and 2 (1), k6 1 and 2 (2 each), and ktor 1 and 2 (3 each). No session showed a limit message, and 24.4 GB was available, so at 06:47 to 06:48 the `r2-er-2b` repair round, sessions 1 and 2 of googleworkspace/cli, heroui and faker, and the inventories of aurora-rss-reader, pentagi and picoclaw started, 12 authors in all. The highlight.js and k6 proofs started at once; the ktor and javalin proofs run Java builds and wait for more free memory. Claude Code stopped a background wait shell at 05:58 when memory ran low, so a recurring scheduler tick now drives the launches and proofs.

The brief text does not change. `r2-repos.json` (now `5ea4ecfd7214749a`) gives `um` and `gr` the commands the hand-built venvs used. Each venv holds the package installed from its own checkout.

Its proof, `r2-er-2b-r1`, failed in three tasks, none from a task defect: `er-f6-a1`'s hidden test hit vitest's 5-second limit at fix, `er-f5-teach` could not find `minimatch`, which its lockfile holds, and `er-f7-teach`'s `pnpm install` stopped on `os error 183`. Repair round 2 started at 07:19.
- **Round 2 pair reading.** `pairs/pairs-input.mjs` (sha256 now `422ca17f8cad0fd2`) also takes `--sessions <list>`, since round 2 folders hold sessions cut by the limit. The first round 2 reading takes github-mcp-server (`r2-gm-1c`, `r2-gm-2`) and k6 (`r2-k6-1`, `r2-k6-2`), input `pairs-input-r2-1.json` (`7e173b052f791167`). Its session 3 (kept rules 9 to 12 of 18 and the `logger` template) is filled for margin and runs after the inventories still waiting.
- **Probes wait for a quiet box.** `s3x-prescreen.ps1` runs `C:/z0-runs/s7/box-check.sh` first. At 07:25 its median bare launch was 326 ms against the 150 ms limit, with CPU at 90% from 12 author sessions and 4 proofs, 44 hours after a reboot. Authoring decides how many repositories qualify, so it keeps its 12 slots. Pair readings and combines go on as each repository is proved, and the A0 probes, leak probes and screens start once the check passes.
- **A clone lock in a proof.** `r2-hl-2`'s proof passed 11 of 12 tasks. In the 12th, `hl-f6-screen`, the proof's own `git clone --shared` stopped on `error replacing .../packed-refs: Permission denied` before any task command ran. This is the Windows scan lock that the runner retries for loose objects (`exec.mjs:100`), but here it hit a clone's `packed-refs`. `prove.mjs` (sha256 now `ed7544ad68f71e35`) retries that clone up to 3 times after it removes the partial folder, and counts each retry in `transientLocks`. `r2-hl-2` is proved again as `r2-hl-2-p2`, all 12 tasks, from 07:30. The runner's own fetch into a cell has the same exposure, and that is a question for the scored run.
- **A void pair reading.** The first round 2 reader's brief added an example that the rule above does not have: two bullets of one checklist count as one convention. Under it, the reader flagged gm-f2 and gm-f5 as parts of the checklist that holds gm-f1 (`copilot-instructions.md:25-31`, lint, test, generate-docs in that order), and kept all four k6 families. Stage 3's third reading kept checklist steps as independent (rf-f5 and rf-f9 above), so that verdict (`pairs-verdict-r2-1-void.json`, `f68ab85a5d79da60`) is void. The brief now holds the rule's own words (`pairs-brief-r2.md`, `6b5aa115847a0723`), and one new reader, who has not seen the void verdict, reads the same 9 pairs. Its verdict stands, whatever it says.
- **First round 2 pair verdict.** The new reader flagged no pair and named 5 close calls, all kept (`pairs-verdict-r2-1.json`, `846d61682520cb97`). The three github-mcp-server families sit in one ordered list (`copilot-instructions.md:25-31`), but each asks for a different thing: the lint gate, the test command and a generated README. So github-mcp-server keeps gm-f1, gm-f2 and gm-f5, and its session 3 stays a margin. `combined/gm-r2a` (3 families, 11 tasks) and `combined/k6-r2a` (k6-f1, f2, f5 and f6, 12 tasks) passed schema, sources, phrases and stubs.
- **One lock retry for the pipeline's git.** The scan lock stopped a third script today: k6's first combine failed on `unable to write file .git/objects/...: Permission denied`. The fill, the proof and the combine each called git with no retry. `combine.mjs` (sha256 now `9ce51ad8e62fa1c5`) and `merge-setn.mjs` (now `5fcbb8407e7124de`) run git through the runner's own `retryObjectLock`. The partial `combined/k6-r2a` was removed and the combine then passed.

The clone retry in `prove.mjs` (sha256 now `2bcb975e8104f792`) now covers any such lock inside the new clone's `.git`, and `r2-fk-2` is proved again as `r2-fk-2-p2`.

The A0 probe runs A0 alone and the screen runs A0 and A4, so neither runs a hook. `s3x-prescreen.ps1` (sha256 now `0fa96b4e0eb64c6b`) runs the check only for the leak probe, `s3x-screen.ps1` (now `478dbf8441aec980`) no longer runs it, and `s3x-run.ps1` keeps it. - **The batch 4 gate starts.** At 08:52 the screen had kept 50 of its 80. `b4/mkqueue.py` (sha256 `44ebfb6d92588747`) queues each kept repository after a review of its GitHub description and top-level files, recorded in `b4/review.json` (`8c880457ca7be777`). Of the first 50, shiaho777/web-to-app fails the licence rule (Unlicense), and alirezarezvani/claude-skills is out as a product of agent instruction files ("380 Claude Code skills & agent skills & plugins"). The other 48 are queued (`b4/queue.json`, `c801c7d1a33f3559`), and later ones join as the screen keeps them. Four Opus workers gate indices 1 to 48, split by index modulo 4, from `gate2-brief.md` (`dbfb0b8a38513061`). Since four workers share `b4/testgate.json`, `b4/run.py` (`47d84f48fe621f6e`) writes it under a lock file, and the workers record counts with its `record` phase, never by hand.

- **One timing reading for every session, a correction.** The 14:19 and 14:26 entries read the timed rerun two ways. The rule of "Timing under load" is per task: a task flagged on time gets exactly one more timed run, and that run stands. So a task's verdict comes from its first proof run that is not void (a lock, or the C compiler missing from the first `r2-gw-2` proof, voids a run); if that run has a time flag, its one timed rerun decides. A later full run, made for other tasks, does not judge it again. The 14:26 entry follows this. The 14:19 entry did not: ol-f1-teach passed with no flag in `r2-ol-1`, so its flags in `r2-ol-1-p2` (180 s) and `r2-ol-1-p3` (189 s) do not count, and ol-f1 stays. The 11:46 entry's rerun for it was not needed. Under this reading every other session keeps the verdict already recorded: ez-1, ez-2, hu-1 and pg-1 as above, and gw-2, where gw-f5-teach's first valid run (`r2-gw-2-p2`, 205 s) had its timed rerun pass in `r2-gw-2-p3`, so its flags in `-p4` (141 s) and `-p5` (126 s) do not count.

Both er set N failures fit: each proof had one or two os error 183 installs and one or two installs missing a package (`magic-string` under vitest, for one). Every arm of the scored run gets the same pnpm. The screens of er and hu ran on 12.3.4. There a failed install voids the session on setup, but a silently missing package can change a screen session's outcome, and the screen records do not show it. `prove.mjs` (sha256 `5c2b5cbe863717d7`) now runs a failed setup once more from a fresh checkout, as the runner's `stageTask` has done since `00ce7f4a`. er's third set N proof, `proofs/er-r2s-setn-p3`, started at 21:19 on both changes.

The set N rule keeps the first 6 tasks that survive the merge, so a task that fails its set N proof leaves the merge like a dropped one: `merge-setn.mjs` (sha 4fc20f1480b29a73) takes a new `--failed` list, which must name a candidate. `merged/jv-r2sb` holds jv-n1, n2, n4 to n7 (shortfall 0) and passed the four checks; its set N proof (maven) started at 03:20. jv-n1, n2 and n4 to n6 keep their verdicts from `jv-r2s-setn`, so only jv-n7 is judged new. kt's set N author started after the rerun ended.
- **08:06. jv is ready; the weekly limit stops new sessions.** `proofs/jv-r2sb-setn` passed with no task problem, and jv-n7 passed unflagged, so jv (jv-f1, f2, f3) has its 6 proved set N tasks and is the fourteenth ready repository. qd's porter, the first run with the `spawnTree` timeout, kept 9 after 56 attempts (161 commits seen; 45 revert conflicts, 2 base passes) with no EPERM. kt's set N author `r2-kt-n` (44 requests) gave no request for any of its 4 ported tasks, because each one's tests call API the change adds, and wrote kt-a1 to a4. A trial merge with all four kt families kept leaves kt 2 short, so the top-up `r2-kt-nb` (8 minus 4, so 4 new tasks) started at 03:41. It ended with status 1 after 2 requests: `Z0_SUB_TOKEN` hit its weekly limit, which resets on 26 October at 04:00 (London), and `Z0_SUB_TOKEN_2` is limited until 24 October. Every step left needs a session: qd's screen, kt's second screen, qd's set N author and kt's top-up. Nothing more starts until a limit resets.
- **09:36. The runner retry is in the frozen build.** At Keith's go (09:05), `z0/r2-freeze` merged `f556d9974` from `z0/runner-unc` as `2fcda7b39`, and `pins-r2.json`'s `hippoCommit` is now that commit (the old file is `pins-r2.pre-merge.json` in the session scratchpad); prereg item 2 has the line. The merge changes only `ab-run.mjs`, `exec.mjs`, `runs.mjs` and `tests/token-eval-z0-exec.test.ts`. The dist hash is still `b940a448`, all 13 files of `z0-r2-freeze/analyzer.sha256` pass `sha256sum -c`, and `src/` is the tag's. The eval suite passed on the merged tree (161 tests, 1 skipped, as at the tag), and the tree stayed clean. The teach-template command of item 4 finds one line on the tag and on the merge, a context line of `src/capture/command.ts` that the diff does not add. So the leak probe can run on the frozen build: 14 ready repositories, 40 kept families, 160 sessions (A1 and A2 on seeds 1 and 2), from 24 October on `Z0_SUB_TOKEN_2`, each start gated by the box check.

## The count reached and the early start, 2026-10-24

- **08:05. The leak probe started.** `Z0_SUB_TOKEN_2` had quota again, and the box check passed on its second try (median 49 ms; the first read 283 ms). The session one-shot cron of 21 October died with its session, so nothing had started. The first session (au-f2 teach, A1 seed 1) ended normally, and A2's first session landed all 3 hippo hooks (slowest 315 ms, `s7/hook-check.mjs`). The leak launcher starts the 14 repositories 3 at a time. kt's top-up author (`r2-kt-nc`) and qd's set N author (`r2-qd-n`, 9 ported, 0 new) started at 08:10 and 08:11. qd's screen goes before kt's second screen, since only qd's screen can change the count. A disk cleanup on 23 October deleted batch 2, 3 and 4 clones, finished session work trees, proof scratch folders, and the NuGet and Julia scored caches. Every `merged/<key>/repo`, batch 4's `src/` and `snap/`, and every record survived.
- **gr does not qualify at 2 families.** Both of gr's kept families are template families (gr-f1, gr-f5), and set R takes at most 1 (Split, above). So 13 of the 14 ready repositories qualify at 2 families: au, er, fk, gm, gz, hl, hu, jv, ol, pc, pg, pm and pu. kt (kt-f1, f5) and qd (qd-f1, f2, if its screen keeps both) are maintainer families, so 15 is the most batches 2 and 3 can give.
- **The sizer at the count reached.** On the frozen sizing inputs, at a 15-point minimum effect (`z0-r2-freeze/sizes-count-1024-f2.json`, families capped at 2 a repository): 14 repositories fit no row (H1 win 0.785, tie 0.730), and 15 fit none either (win 0.820, tie 0.788). At 16 (`sizes-count-1024-f3.json`) the chosen row is 2 families a repository, H1 win 0.843 and tie 0.821 at the point estimates, 0.611 and 0.447 at the bounds, level held, about 3,949 sessions. A 3-family design fits at 14 (win 0.928), but only 8 repositories have 3 usable families. So the scored run needs 16 qualifying repositories at 2 families, and at least one must come from batch 4.
- **Keith's go: 16 first.** Asked whether to run at 16 or author batch 4 toward 30 first, Keith said to decide what is best and run the 16 first. Batch 4 authoring opens now on its tier 1 in its order, until 16 qualify. Its sources and snapshots survived the cleanup.
- **Early start.** At 16 and above, the design's families a repository is 2. So a qualifying repository's run spec is the same whatever the final count: its 2 lowest-numbered kept families (at most 1 template) and its first 6 set N tasks. Each of the 13 starts wave 1 (seeds 1 to 3, arms A0, A1, A2, A4 and A5, one runner a seed) once its leak probe ends with no hit. The seed rule still reads every repository's wave 1 before any wave 2. If fewer than 16 qualify, those runs stay unscored, as item 8 says. This start was set before any scored session ran.
- **08:38. kt's top-up count was wrong, so `r2-kt-nd` writes 8.** `r2-kt-nc` ended with status 0 after 43 requests. Like `r2-kt-n`, it gave no request for any of kt's 4 ported tasks and wrote 4 new tasks, again numbered kt-a1 to a4. A trial merge with it (`C:/z0-runs/t/kt-all`, all four kt families kept, and `kt-f15`, kt-f1 and f5 only) chose kt-a1 to a4 and was 2 short either way. The cause is the top-up count. "8 minus usable" (pm, pu) counts ported tasks that the top-up author asks for again. kt's 4 usable tasks were authored by `r2-kt-n`, and `merge-setn.mjs` reads only the named author's folder, so they never carry over. So the top-up must write 8 minus the ported tasks it can use, here 8. `r2-kt-nd` (8 new) started at 08:38.

At `--n 5` every arm gets exactly 1 pair in every folder, 16 per arm and 80 in all, which is "`--n` per folder in proportion to its arms" (Amendment 4 item 5) taken literally. The removed draws can be rebuilt exactly with `--seed 20261028 --n 2`. One limit: with 1 pair per arm, the draw's split by saved verdict inside an arm (`proportional`, largest remainder) takes that arm's majority verdict in each folder.

H1 and G2 read only first checks (prereg 151: "its state at the end of its first session, before any correction"); final checks feed `resolved`. It runs with `-Xmx15760m` (a quarter of RAM), ParallelGC, and a 2 h idle life (`--daemon-autoshutdownIdleSeconds=7200`), and survives from cell to cell. `jcmd GC.heap_info` showed its old generation 99% full at 5.9 GB; after `jcmd GC.run` (a 1.3 s pause, no effect on build output) the old generation held 27 MB. ParallelGC kept the 6 GB committed, so the working set stayed at 8.5 GB. Stopping the daemon mid-compile could turn a cell's check into a false fail, so it stays up while jv runs. `kdaemon-watch.ps1` (scratchpad, detached, log `kdaemon.log`) runs `GC.run` every 10 min while jv's regrade lives, which keeps the old generation from filling and growing. Exploratory, after the Amendment 9 read: the context fallback costs nothing, and the next step named in Amendment 9 is withdrawn.** This splits set N A2 sessions from the Amendment 9 reruns against Amendment 8's A1 by task (`scratchpad/a9-split.mjs`, 278 pairs, pooled `costUsd`). Sessions where the hook injected nothing and the agent ran `hippo context` cost 1.023 of A1 (14 pairs). Sessions with no injection and no call cost 1.036 (19 pairs). The agent ran the fallback in the same Bash call as `ls` and `git status`, and 0 of the 21 fallback calls returned a memory entry (`a9-ctx-ret.mjs`). So a marker for an empty hook result would save nothing, and that next step is withdrawn. Sessions where the hook injected text cost 1.058 (237 pairs, mean 1,657 characters). Of the 252 injections, 249 carry a Session Handoff pointer to the previous task's session, and 8 sessions that ran a hippo command after a short injection cost 1.275. These subgroups are small and not pre-registered; they point at the handoff pointer and the injected block size, not at agent calls.
